# RefactorKit 0.8.0 completion register

Observed: 2026-11-20. Target: the full catalogue in the
[0.8.0 plan](v0.8.0-plan.md), Linux x86-64 with explicitly configured Clang,
clangd and clang-format 22.1.8. The working source/build version is 0.8.0, API 0.2.

This satellite records implementation findings and outstanding work. The
[ARC42 appendix](../arc42/appendix-requirements.adoc) owns integrated requirement
status. A bounded passing Story does not qualify its entire planning row.
No whole-release publication or independent acceptance, CI execution, downloaded
release artifact or unmeasured platform is marked complete by this register.

both release plans on C11 and C17 for Linux x86-64 in 0.8.0 and multihost
qualification in 1.0.0. All 15 historical 0.7.0 policy controls passed.

The same archive passed 156 additional C cases across CLI, LSP, daemon and
MCP. Successful operations were previewed without writes, applied through
the managed transaction and rolled back byte for byte. Refused operations
preserved source bytes without creating a transaction journal.

| Row | Passing cases | Verified scope |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| C08 | 32 | Move a source, shared header or component tree with captured CMake bindings. Verify exact include and compilation-database updates where affected. Reject occupied paths, computed includes, dynamic bindings, generated sources and uncovered translation units. Native execution passed before and after all three moves. |
| C09 | 24 | Rename two explicitly mapped API symbols across a header and two translation units in C11 and C17. Preserve comments, strings and an unrelated same-prefix symbol. Reject occupied and duplicate destinations and unqualified C++/Objective-C consumers. Native execution passed before and after the C11 and C17 edits. |
| C10 | 36 | Rename, add and reorder parameters. Reject side-effecting reorder, address-taking in add and reorder, variadic and old-style reorder, and unproven external consumers in rename. Native execution matched before and after all three positive fixtures. |
| C11 | 16 | Extract a scalar expression and a straight-line statement selection. Reject side-effecting selections. Native execution matched before and after both positive fixtures. |
| C12 | 16 | Inline a direct function call and a local scalar. Reject the bounded side-effect and volatile cases. Native execution matched before and after both positive fixtures. |
| C13 | 16 | Delete an unused static function or object. Reject address-taking and unproven public consumers. |
| C14 | 16 | Reorder independent include declarations and remove a duplicate guarded include. Reject typedef-dependent ordering and multiple repeated groups. |

Reports are retained under `.refactorkit/runs/v080-current-package-c08-moves-20261120-r002/`
and the corresponding `c09-prefix`, `c10-signature`, `c11-extraction`,
`c12-inline`, `c13-safe-delete` and `c14-includes` runs for the same archive.

### Integrated 0.8.0 source and local qualification — 2026-11-19

The working source and build now use 0.8.0 with API 0.2. The integrated changes
include Gradle build ownership and resynchronization, Java-to-Kotlin binding,
native C relocation and LSP coverage, and the SPDX, recovery and downloaded
asset checks. Focused results and source hashes are recorded in
`.refactorkit/runs/v080-current-integration-20261119-r007/`. RefactorKit's IDE
consumer received the integrated Java and Kotlin results.

The existing candidate ZIP passed all 40 golden requests with the `c-inline`
native expected image corrected in a disposable copy. The separate structural
inline golden passed against its original expected image. The release profile
bootstrap and JDK 25 diagnostics passed with the selected LTS inputs. The run
record contains the remaining checks and their boundaries. The local rehearsal
uses the provisional native golden image and measures the default regression
suite on Linux x86-64.

At the time of this run the proposed fixture changes still required approval.
The later `c-inline` correction is recorded above. Independent acceptance,
distribution licensing, the exact-source CI run and verification of the
published download remain open.

### Full-root interruption and remote baseline readback — 2026-11-19

The fresh default `check goldenTest --continue --rerun-tasks` run
`.refactorkit/runs/v080-current-root-20261119-r005/` was externally interrupted.
Both its tool handle and recorded process are gone; the host process inspection
found no remaining qualification JVM. All 2029 source identities and Git status
match before/after. Completed reports prove 749 passing checks (C 741 plus 8 CLI
navigation/query cases), with 1,260 registered but unselected cases in completed
focused task reports. The unfinished main CLI binary results are preserved and
are not counted as passed scenarios. This is **incomplete**, with no whole-root
PASS; a new current-source run is required after implementation integration.

Direct read-only GitHub API access now verifies the published 0.7.0 asset checksum
against its historical acceptance document. The observed latest CI run 36292990906
is on HEAD 5e082d15, predates the uncommitted changes, and has overall failure.
Its build and four focused Linux jobs passed, but no Linux runtime job exists
among its 20 jobs. These historical results do not qualify the present 0.8 source.
No public 0.8.0 release is listed; unauthenticated access cannot settle draft
visibility. `releases/latest` returns 0.6.2 despite the explicit published 0.7.0
record, so it must not be treated as highest-version authority. Evidence:
`.refactorkit/runs/c17-remote-readback-20261119-r001/audit.json`.
No remote mutation, installation or adoption occurred.
See [ARC42 authority](../arc42/appendix-requirements.adoc#c17-root-interruption-remote-baseline-2026-11-19).

### Earlier ancestor identity correction — 2026-11-19

The separate69a7 source-ownership archive passes24 packaged BDD cases/96 steps,
10 strict daemon/MCP envelopes and SPDX schema/inventory, but its first direct
diagnostic query refuses with java.overlay.analysisUnavailable. Bounded JVM logs
identify OwnedSourceCaptureException INPUT_CHANGED at/tmp during ancestry
descent and post-query scanning. The failed native matrix is retained;69a7 is
WITHHELD, not handed off as qualified. This trace does not establish the cause
of the older unknown acquisition observation.

A separate filesystem-boundary Story deterministically interleaves real NIO

Maven/Gradle acceptance of source-set compiler identity/options, declared source/API

and matching JDT parser/API analysis.

is claimed. The IDE consumer reports that wizard-generated Maven projects for

IDE Java provider progress, 2026-10-22: the public Maven `project.context` JPMS
provider parses `module-info.java` with its real unit name and fails closed when the
strict v2 DTO omits descriptor directives. A descriptor with `opens`, `uses`,
`provides`, or another unprojected directive retains supported metadata but reports
JPMS `partial`; `requiredFacts: [JPMS]` refuses with
`java.context.jpmsDescriptorIncomplete`. The Story passes one scenario and preserves
workspace bytes without running build/debugger code. Receipt and analyzer inventories
are in `.refactorkit/runs/java-jpms-descriptor-completeness-r001/`. This records the
initial provider-only state; the bounded JDT module-path follow-up is recorded below.

Optional JPMS acceptance, 2026-10-23: `project.context` now separates build-model
metadata availability from JDT semantic readiness. A supported metadata-only request
remains `available` when an unprojected `module-info.java` directive makes JPMS
`partial`; it omits JDT analysis in that case, and requiring JPMS still refuses with
`java.context.jpmsDescriptorIncomplete`. Ordinary classpath analysis remains enabled
when partial JPMS evidence contains no module descriptor or module-path entry, while
complete named-module analysis is unchanged. The exact Stories pass for the six-case
SDK platform declaration matrix, partial descriptor/required-fact refusal, complete
Maven JPMS metadata and Maven JPMS export semantics. The slice compiled with JDK 21;
receipt and isolated logs are under
`.refactorkit/runs/java-optional-jpms-context-r001/`.

A second provider Story now changes `module-info.java` after `project.open` and
verifies public `project.context` refuses the old snapshot with
`java.context.snapshotStale` before publishing module facts. It passes one scenario
and four steps; receipt and pre/post inventories are in
`.refactorkit/runs/java-jpms-stale-descriptor-r001/`.

A third Story changes a compiler-configured Maven module-path JAR after
`project.open`. The old snapshot is refused with `java.context.snapshotStale` before
module facts are published. It passes one scenario and four steps; receipt and
pre/post inventories are in
`.refactorkit/runs/java-jpms-stale-module-path-r001/`. At that point, complete
module-path resolution, Gradle stale-artifact checks, and separate-module JDT
navigation remained open; subsequent bounded Gradle acceptance closes the stale
artifact case and the Maven/Gradle semantic follow-up below closes the tested JDT path.

A fourth Story adds the positive Maven module-path case: it compiles a real named
dependency JAR and verifies complete JPMS fact availability, exact module name and
file fingerprint, descriptor identity, `requires` modifiers, qualified exports and
descriptor hash. Required JPMS facts are not refused; that metadata-only response
recorded overall `partial` with no selected source before JDT consumed the separate
path. It passes one scenario and four steps; receipt and pre/post inventories are in
`.refactorkit/runs/java-jpms-positive-module-path-r001/`. The historical response is
retained as captured evidence.

A fifth Story covers the positive Gradle module-path case after explicit sync. It
compiles a real named dependency JAR and verifies complete JPMS fact availability,
exact module identity and fingerprint, descriptor identity/directives/hash, the
unchanged v2 response, and preserved Java/build input bytes. The acceptance exposed
that Gradle compiler module-path entries were missing from scanner
`ClasspathEvidence`; the scanner now captures those entries. Required JPMS is not
refused; that metadata-only response recorded overall `partial` with no selected
source before JDT consumed the separate module path. It passes one scenario and four
steps; receipt and pre/post inventories are in
`.refactorkit/runs/java-jpms-gradle-positive-module-path-r001/`. The historical
response is retained as captured evidence.

A sixth Story qualifies stale Gradle module-path artifact handling. It explicitly
synchronizes the fixture, changes the configured modular JAR, then requests JPMS using
the synchronized snapshot. The provider returns `java.context.snapshotStale`, changes
the snapshot hash, and publishes no module facts; the context request preserves the
post-mutation fixture bytes. It passes one scenario and four steps; the hash-bound
receipt and pre/post glue inventories are in
`.refactorkit/runs/java-jpms-gradle-stale-module-path-r001/`. This closes the bounded
literal Gradle compiler module-path staleness case.

The 2026-10-22 JDT JPMS follow-up now passes separate Maven and Gradle semantic
Stories. The resolver supplies the snapshot-bound module path independently from the
ordinary classpath, associates the complete source descriptor with its exact root,
and honors `requires`/`exports`: the exported dependency type binds and the hidden
package remains unresolved. Separate metadata Stories verify complete required-JPMS
responses as overall `available` with JDT analysis and no selected source. All four
stories pass one isolated scenario/four steps; their post-edit inventories report no
ambiguity or duplicate expressions. Receipts and reports are in
`.refactorkit/runs/java-jpms-gradle-semantic-context-r001/`,
`.refactorkit/runs/java-jpms-gradle-context-available-r001/`,
`.refactorkit/runs/java-jpms-maven-semantic-context-r001/` and
`.refactorkit/runs/java-jpms-maven-context-available-r001/`. This closes only the
bounded RefactorKit JDT route. Broader module-path ownership/aggregation and native
IDE consumer navigation remain open; the latter is outside this repository's
implementation scope and remains an integration acceptance gate.

across both units, and rollback restores all original source/header/database bytes.

reported separately. No semantic-equivalence claim or broader C14/C16 closure is
made.

14 scenarios/56 steps with zero failures, errors, or skips. Pre-edit inventory

gate is separate evidence.

owner, checks that the supplied `WorkspaceEdit` is exactly the planner's header,

receipt and inventories are in
`.refactorkit/runs/cmake-shared-header-managed-apply-r001/`; the pre-edit analyzer
found 3 exact reuses and 1 new step, and post-edit found 4 exact reuses, zero

rerun of the existing C-source multi-TU outline also passes all three examples:
the leaf move applies and rolls back, while an inbound source include and a
path-sensitive moved source refuse with `c.compilationContextUnavailable` before
WAL. The guard now preserves that source-only leaf policy while sharing the
consumer-rewrite path with `.h` moves; missing preview leases also fail as missing
captured CMake context rather than as runtime drift. Its exact scenario receipt
and logs are in `.refactorkit/runs/cmake-multi-tu-leaf-refusal-r001/`. This evidence
does not qualify include-root `-I`/`target_include_directories`, tree moves,
generated/external or macro-sensitive headers, arbitrary consumers, linking,

## Regression work in progress

The first unfiltered `check goldenTest` baseline failed in the daemon/MCP
source-root move Story because discovery discarded an explicitly declared Maven
module named `target`. A separate six-case Story now proves direct/nested
output-named modules and selected/inactive profiles. Declaration-driven discovery
preserves the module and captures its descriptor without admitting undeclared
output copies. The subsequent unfiltered run passed C (167), CLI (265), core
(125), daemon (63), Java (274), MCP (32) and golden (33) cases. CLI retained
355 exclusions and Java five skips. It stopped at JVM with 17 failures and five
skips, including repeated `jspawnhelper` reports of Java 21.0.11 versus installed
21.0.12.1. A fresh parent/child Java probe succeeds on 21.0.12.1. Fresh retries of
the two affected JVM classes pass all 54 tests with zero skips; the 17 failures
remain preserved as environment-drift evidence. Later-module reports from prior
runs are not fresh evidence for this run. The separate post-C02 regression
passes 738 cases across C, daemon, MCP, Kotlin, LSP, Tree-sitter, TypeScript,
web importer and golden, with ten LSP skips. The CLI was rebuilt. This does not
make the earlier interrupted `check` a successful unfiltered run.

Local receipt, installed-analyzer inventory and RED/GREEN report copies:
`.refactorkit/runs/java-scan-declared-output-r001/`. These ignored local artifacts
support development; they are not independent release acceptance.

A later offline `check goldenTest` run with the pinned Clang 22.1.8 toolchain
passed the C module, then reached a stale CLI capability-truth assertion:
`changeSignature.removeParameter` is now advertised as stable, managed and
compiler-backed, while the rename Story still expected it to be proposal-only.
The oracle is corrected and its exact rename Story passes; the focused XML shows
one selected scenario passed and six siblings skipped. The full CLI suite and
`check goldenTest` were not rerun to completion, so this is not a green release
gate. The hash-bound RED/GREEN receipt is in
`.refactorkit/runs/java-capability-truth-remove-parameter-authority-r001/`.

### C17 full-worktree attempt — 2026-10-24

The exact-source command
`JAVA_HOME=/usr/lib/jvm/java-21-openjdk REFACTORKIT_C_QUALIFICATION_TOOLCHAIN=<root> ./gradlew --offline --no-daemon --no-watch-fs --max-workers=4 check goldenTest`
passed the JDK guard only after selecting JDK 21. The first attempt under JDK 25
stopped before Gradle tasks, as required by the repository build guard.

The initial JDK 21 attempt used the existing local toolchain directory whose tools
reported version 22.1.8 but did not match the release archive: its `clang`,
`clangd` and `clang-format` hashes were respectively `6eff476f…`, `f5189aee…`
and `ebf64571…`, and its resource directory contained 413 files. The official
1,938,859,476-byte archive already cached at
`/tmp/refactorkit-llvm-22.1.8/LLVM-22.1.8-Linux-X64.tar.xz` matches the pinned
SHA-256 `df0e1ecf…`; its unmodified executables match the three hashes in
`scripts/prepare-clang-qualification-toolchain.sh`, and its Clang 22 resource
directory contains 385 files. A private, non-installed test root is staged at
`/tmp/refactorkit-llvm-22.1.8/qualified/` for the corrected rerun. Hashes and
command output are retained under
`.refactorkit/runs/c17-full-worktree-check-r001-current/`.

Against the mismatched local root, the C module passed 301/301 tests. The CLI
suite recorded 1,183 tests, 132 failures, 381 skips and no XML-reported errors;
Gradle then aborted `Gradle Test Executor 6` with `Required array size too large`
after 38m27s. The failure groups include binary/resource identity mismatches,
missing `/usr/bin/clang-22` on this host, and downstream runtime/CMake assertions.
CLI test glue still contains hard-coded `/usr/bin/clang-22`, `/usr/bin/clangd` or
`/usr/bin/clang-format` paths instead of consistently using the admitted
`REFACTORKIT_C_QUALIFICATION_TOOLCHAIN`; the current source inventory is retained
with the focused follow-up below. No later-module result from an older run is
carried forward as evidence for this attempt. Several SpotBugs invocations
reported exit code 1; their findings remain untriaged. This attempt is diagnostic
evidence only and is not a C17 pass.

### C17 exact LLVM provisioning follow-up — 2026-10-24

The full-check failure exposed a mismatch between the CI provisioner and the
symlink-safe glibc runtime resolver: the script created `usr` as a symlink to the
extracted archive, which makes runtime search-profile capture refuse the otherwise
correct binaries. The provisioner now extracts the hash-pinned members directly
under a real `${toolchain_root}/usr`, strips the archive's single top-level
directory, and rejects symbolic directory ancestors before publishing the root.

The tests-only RED ran the existing six-case
`REQ-C-CLANG-RUNTIME-DISCOVERY-001` Story against the old symlink-shaped official
archive root: 3/6 examples failed because the reference runtime could not be
captured. GREEN ran the same Story against the exact official archive staged by
the corrected script: 6/6 passed. The script verified the archive SHA-256 and
all three executable hashes; its resource tree contained 385 files. The complete
C module then passed 301 tests across 49 suites with zero skips, failures or
errors. The cached archive was supplied through a local curl shim, so this proves
the provisioner/extraction behavior without an actual network download; exact-
source CI remains outstanding. The receipt, analyzer inventories, logs, native
evidence and reports are under
`.refactorkit/runs/c17-toolchain-root-provisioning-r001/`.

This closes the observed local `usr` symlink mismatch only. The full CLI suite,
its test-worker crash, SpotBugs findings, exact-source CI and all remaining C02-C17
qualification and release gates remain `actionable`.

provisioner and the resource tree has 385 files. The installed analyzer reports

ambiguity. Pre/post inventories, source manifests, receipt, logs and Cucumber
report are in
`.refactorkit/runs/c17-cli-cmake-path-define-toolchain-r001/`.

This closes one fixture-routing defect only. A fresh scan still finds twenty CLI
test sources containing literal `/usr/bin/clang*` references; their exact paths
are listed in `hardcoded-system-clang-test-files-current.txt`. The full CLI suite
and full-worktree check have not yet been rerun with the official toolchain root;
the previous worker crash and SpotBugs findings remain open.

## C02 launch admission

The six-case operational Story now passes with four new steps in the shared
runner. clangd consumes a private admitted database with the configured compiler,
ignores project configuration, and refuses plugin/response/config/command-string
inputs before launch. Definition resolution proves the required captured define;
project byte preservation and process/private-material cleanup are checked.

During the run, system tooling moved from 22.1.8 to 23.1.1. Cached 22.1.8
packages were verified against the installed CachyOS public keyring and extracted
privately. Their original compiler hash matches the prior 22.1.8 evidence.
Private executable/library RUNPATH derivations, signatures, licenses and 413
resource-input hashes are recorded in
`.refactorkit/runs/v080-clangd-launch-r001/`. These test-only artifacts target the
current x86-64-v4 host; they do not qualify a generic external toolchain or a
shipped archive. The initial six missing-tool setup failures are classified as
setup-unavailable, not semantic RED. Valid RED and GREEN reports are separate.

### C02 clangd initialize timeout/cancellation and descendant cleanup

`REQ-C-CLANGD-LAUNCH-001#a-clangd-initialize-timeout-terminates-its-descendant-process-tree`
now passes as one exact-identity Story on Linux x86-64 with the pinned LLVM
22.1.8 toolchain. Its fake clangd starts a live child and withholds the initialize
response; the 1.2-second deadline refuses startup, and both recorded PIDs are
confirmed exited before manager shutdown. The C project inputs remain unchanged.
The shared runner inventory reports one runner, no ambiguous bindings and no
duplicate expressions. The fresh pre/post inventory, implementation manifest,
Cucumber/JUnit output and execution log for the current shared-feature hash are in
`.refactorkit/runs/v080-clangd-process-tree-timeout-r005/`. This requalification
finds all four steps as exact reuse; its JUnit report filters four sibling
scenarios. The receipt binds feature SHA-256
`ec49a19409b57352b8bfc564d64926b65edeaf731f01c8cca1965a7f6d43d949`.

This closes only the initialize-timeout/descendant case.

The separate exact-identity scenario
`REQ-C-CLANGD-LAUNCH-001#cancelling-clangd-initialization-closes-its-descendant-process-tree`
also passes. It starts `ClangdSemanticClient.start` asynchronously, waits until the
fake server and its child are both live, then closes the client during the pending
initialize handshake. Both processes exit and manager provenance is empty before
manager shutdown; project inputs are unchanged. The pre-edit inventory found two
new steps, two exact reuses and no ambiguity. The post-edit inventory has four
exact reuses, one runner, no ambiguity and no duplicate expressions. One active
scenario/four steps pass; the JUnit report records four sibling scenarios as
skipped by the exact tag filter. Final-feature evidence is in

## C10 pure parameter-reordering policy

The user explicitly approved Kotest 5.9.1 for Kotlin Spec BDD. The two approved
C-module test dependencies resolve and the engine executes on the existing
Kotlin 2.0.21 / Java 21 build. No production dependency or framework plugin is
added. The initial absent-subject compilation and missing offline runtime JARs
are preserved as setup evidence, not semantic RED.

Eight focused specifications then fail against a contract-only implementation
and pass against the pure policy. The association oracle covers every
nonidentity permutation of three parameters across two calls. Typed refusals
retain invalid mappings, bounded input, no-op requests, unknown/indirect/external
families, wrong arity and unavailable/observable argument effects. Accepted
orders are immutable copies. The
[requirement satellite](../requirements/c-parameter-reordering.md) owns these
pure rules; evidence is in
`.refactorkit/runs/v080-parameter-reorder-policy-r001/`.

This internal policy consumes supplied facts and emits a decision. It does not
produce compiler evidence or a patch, advertise a new command, or close C10.
Canonical compiler facts, declaration/direct-call edits and managed transport
qualification remain required; added parameters remain a separate positive target.

## C04/C10 pure function-family facts

Thirteen approved Kotest specifications pass against a locally authored C source
and captured Clang 22.1.8 AST. The fixture normalizes only its filename and opaque
pointer IDs, retaining declaration/reference links and byte coordinates. Exact
prototype, definition and call argument slices are asserted independently; an
unrelated same-name variable stays outside the family.

The pure decoder follows complete `previousDecl` identities, validates owned
UTF-8 byte ranges, retains address-taking/external/unknown consumer dispositions
and admits argument effects only from explicitly supported AST forms. Assembly,
linker aliases or an unbound indirect call prevent closed-family evidence.
Missing/cyclic/duplicate identities, macros, unowned ranges, unsupported
signatures and traversal limits refuse. Returned collections are immutable.

Ten valid semantic failures precede the initial implementation; one recursive
fixture mutator incorrectly selected abbreviated reference records and is
corrected separately. The additional consumer-closure specifications reproduce
two semantic RED failures with eleven passing controls. All thirteen now pass.
The [requirement satellite](../requirements/c-function-family-facts.md) owns the
pure decoding contract. Provenance, admission and report copies are in
`.refactorkit/runs/v080-function-family-facts-r001/`.

This first cell supports named integer parameters in one owned source file.
Captured fixture decoding does not qualify an admitted compiler execution,
source/header/tool input closure, staged diagnostics or a refactoring operation.
Those integrations and full C04/C10 acceptance remain open.

## C02 Pure Per-Object glibc Search Proposals

`REQ-C-GLIBC-SEARCH-001` adds six approved Kotest 5.9.1 specifications and no
new dependency. Supplied indexed DT_NEEDED occurrences receive immutable,
ordered per-object RUNPATH candidate paths and a typed cache fallback. Only
literal absolute directories and correctly delimited ORIGIN tokens at an
alias-free physical origin are supported. Raw expanded spelling and repetitions
remain; the proposal does not normalize away intermediate directories, inherit
parent RUNPATH, infer default paths or choose filesystem objects. RPATH,
NODEFLIB, empty/relative entries, unknown tokens and invalid/oversized UTF-8
refuse explicitly. Cache ambiguity/unavailability remains typed after candidate
paths, so it cannot preempt a valid earlier RUNPATH hit.

## C02 Alias-Free Raw Origin Correction

Preparing actual recursive resolution exposes a valid library selector reached
through the compiler's `ORIGIN/../lib`. Its alias-free raw spelling differs from
its normalized captured physical path. The corrected requirement allows ORIGIN
expansion from that physical parent while preserving original graph spelling;
aliases and genuinely different physical origins still refuse. One corrected

## C02 Native Reference-Profile glibc Runtime Resolution

`REQ-C-GLIBC-RESOLUTION-001` binds one six-case Story, fresh receipt, installed

stay reported. The separate requirement-driven raw-origin correction is an
explicit five-file Spec prerequisite, not hidden inside this Story budget.

The read-only library resolver captures executable/interpreter, uses each
object's indexed RUNPATH/cache proposals in breadth-first order, reuses loaded
names, captures selected ELF/aliases and delegates graph composition to the pure
manifest. Directory inspection preserves raw intermediate traversal and refuses
aliases. It retains failed-directory/candidate searches and hardware-directory
absence; unsupported hardware/default/cache/RPATH profiles never become guessed
selection. Object/edge/observation/depth and per-file/per-traversal byte budgets
bound acquisition and verification. Selected objects, search absences and cache/
preload are rechecked before return; later currency resolves original selectors
again and compares the complete graph/cache/search tuple. No native tool executes
in production capture, and locking/race closure or launch admission is not claimed.

Three controls independently match actual private 22.1.8 compiler/server/formatter
loader observations: sixteen objects with 44/46/44 edges. Derivation-ledger tool
hashes and dependency research hashes agree; independent read-only ELF/cache/hash
oracles verify metadata, selected paths and indexed edges. Owned cache or libffi
bytes changing revoke currency. An owned cache lacking the libffi candidate
refuses unavailable default-search authority; this is not proof the host has no
library. Original source/host data stays intact, with fixture mutations explicit.
All six first native implementation cases pass. Fresh C (290, including 124
approved Kotest specifications), golden (33) and 30 native cases pass: 353 selected
tests, 410 deliberate CLI exclusions. This is not unfiltered release qualification.

### Current release and IDE regression audit — 2026-10-22

The six-case `REQ-JAVA-PROJECT-CONTEXT-001` source-provenance Story had a stale
schema assertion: the glue expected `java-project-context-v1` while the endpoint emits
the documented `java-project-context-v2`. All six examples failed at that assertion
before the edit. The existing step now checks v2; a forced source build passes all six
examples and 24/24 expanded steps. The current feature SHA-256 remains
`2e273eacaa86c7f9d7f21d48c960ff00f25084428f3992597f4ef12d94eac84b`. Fresh pre/post
inventories, the single-outline receipt and Cucumber report are under
`.refactorkit/runs/java-project-context-v2-fix-r001/`.

The separate six-case `REQ-JAVA-QUERY-CONTEXT-001` Story also expected v1 in its
successful `intelligence.query` context echo, contrary to the API 0.2 contract. Its
existing glue now expects `java-project-context-v2`; all six examples pass with
24/24 steps. Its own exact-scenario receipt, inventories and preserved Cucumber report
are under `.refactorkit/runs/java-query-context-v2-fix-r001/`. These two repairs
reconcile assertions with the documented wire contract only. Provider-qualified
source selection, Maven Failsafe details, complete Gradle compiler options and Maven
release-27 refusal remain separate open gaps under `REQ-JAVA-PROJECT-CONTEXT-001`.

A focused source-built Maven module-rename run exposed and now closes a planner
regression. `JavaRenameMavenModuleStaging.rehydrateSemanticSnapshot` rebased compile and
runtime classpath entries but left `BuildResolvedArtifact` and annotation-processor path
evidence rooted at the staging copy. Reconstructing `BuildSourceSet` therefore violated
its path-ownership invariant and the planner converted the resulting exception into a
fail-closed refusal. Rehydration now rebases both artifact-coordinate evidence and
processor paths alongside their owning classpaths; the temporary exception print was
removed. The direct-library acceptance passes 1/1 scenario and 50/50 steps, and the
source-built CLI SURFACE-002 acceptance passes 1/1 scenario. Hash-bound inventories and
execution evidence are under

authority, script-body execution, settings/plugin resolution, or Gradle build
execution. This establishes fail-closed recognition only; Kotlin script semantic
support remains unavailable.

### IDE native LSP makeStatic action — 2026-10-22

The Java LSP code-action route now offers `makeStatic` as a standard `CodeAction`

RefactorKit LSP mapping gap only; packaged/native IDE acceptance, other requested
transformations, C01-C17 acceptance, and 0.8.0 release qualification remain open.

### IDE Java CLI process-output ceiling — 2026-10-22

The executable CLI now enforces an aggregate 2 MiB UTF-8 budget across stdout and
stderr. It buffers at most the admitted response, discards both streams if a
command exceeds the budget, and emits the bounded `cli.outputLimitExceeded`
diagnostic; `--help` publishes the same limit. `RefactorKitCli.run()` when called
directly in-process is outside this executable transport bound.

`REQ-JAVA-RUNTIME-BOUNDS-001#oversized-cli-output-is-discarded-atomically` passes
one selected scenario with four steps through the existing CLI runner. The child
process searched 100,000 matches whose minimum rendered output exceeds the limit;
the test proves empty stdout on overflow, the bounded diagnostic and exit code,
the help value, and byte-exact normal `--version` output. The pre-edit analyzer
found four required steps and one runner; the final bounded inventory records four
exact reuses, zero new steps, zero duplicates and zero ambiguity. Evidence is in
`.refactorkit/runs/java-cli-output-bounds-r001/`. This closes only the executable
CLI output-ceiling gap. Runtime timeout, process isolation/provenance, other
platforms, independent acceptance and the full C01-C17 / 0.8.0 gates remain open.

### IDE-requested Gradle compiler options in public Java context — 2026-10-22

Explicit Gradle sync now projects literal `JavaCompile.options.compilerArgs` into
the existing per-source-set `compilerOptions` wire records, retaining order and
compile-task/source-set origin. Sensitive values are redacted before the temporary
export. Completeness is withheld when argument providers are present or unavailable,
when a custom Java compile task action could change the arguments, or when bounded
capture is incomplete; required `compiler-options` then refuses with
`java.context.compilerOptionsIncomplete`. The exporter does not evaluate provider
arguments or execute compile tasks, and the existing v2/v3 wire schema is unchanged.

The four-case public Story passes for preview enabled/absent, a provider and a
redacted argument (16/16 steps). A separate four-step Story proves a custom `doFirst`
action is not executed and its fact is refused as partial. Receipts, pre/post glue
inventories and execution evidence are in
`.refactorkit/runs/java-gradle-compiler-options-r001/` and
`.refactorkit/runs/java-gradle-task-action-r001/`. This closes the bounded RefactorKit
Gradle provider gap for Java 25. Broader Maven option recognition, Java 27 and the
IDE's independent context-consumption path remain open; the complete IDE backlog,
C01-C17 and 0.8.0 release qualification are not closed.

## C17 pinned Clang 22.1.8 qualification path — 2026-10-22

The CI build prepares the official LLVM 22.1.8 Linux x86-64 archive before
Gradle tests. The v0.8.x release build uses the same setup behind a v0.8 tag
condition; the historical v0.7.0 steps remain unchanged. The setup checks the
archive SHA-256 `df0e1ecf16caf3489a272a5eea4eec9b0d82878f6477fa309504f918a0006384`,
the `clang-22`, `clangd` and `clang-format` hashes, versions, target and resource
directory, then exports one normalized toolchain root. Shared Gradle test
configuration passes that root to C and CLI test workers. It extracts only the
three tools, `libclang-cpp.so.22.1`, the complete Clang resource directory and
the LLVM license file, then removes the compressed archive. This avoids
unpacking the unused 12 GiB full distribution on the runner. The official
[LLVM 22.1.8 release](https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/releases/tag/llvmorg-22.1.8)
and its [build attestation](https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/attestations/31423841)
are recorded with the local toolchain preview.

The first exact-archive run exposed a missed runtime input: the Clang resource
tree contains a 27,987,190-byte `libflang_rt.runtime.a`, above the previous 8 MiB
per-file bound. Resource capture therefore became unavailable and three C
integration tests refused diagnostics/apply. The pure resource rule now allows
at most 32 MiB per file while keeping the complete-tree ceiling at 128 MiB; its
Kotest Spec admits the observed runtime and 32 MiB boundary and refuses one byte
over. With that bound, the full C suite passes 297 tests with no skips on the
local x86-64 host using the exact archive. The
[requirement](../requirements/clang-resource-directory.md) and ARC42 appendix
record the authority change.

`REQ-C-INTERIM-HOST-001#ci-and-the-v0-8-release-test-c-against-the-attested-clang-toolchain`
passes as one active scenario/case. Its fresh post-edit analyzer report finds
five exact step reuses, zero ambiguous or missing steps, zero runner ambiguity,
49 existing runners and 44 glue roots; it adds no runner. The worker log confirms
that the CLI test JVM receives the exported toolchain path. Receipts, inventory,
toolchain preview and green/red reports are in
`.refactorkit/runs/c17-clang-ci-toolchain-r001/`.

The existing CLI runtime-manifest Story also passes all six cases/24 steps with
the same Gradle forwarding path; its test JVM reports the selected toolchain
root. This checks the CLI boundary locally, but it does not replace the full
GitHub build job.

No GitHub Actions run, tag, release or publication was triggered. C17 still
requires exact-source GitHub CI, full release/runtime qualification, independent
review, provenance/SBOM/checksums and independently executed downloads before
publication.

### C06 same-translation-unit target collision refusal — 2026-10-22

The new exact-selection case exposed that clangd may return edits when the
requested function spelling already exists in the selected translation unit.
`CRefactoringFacade` now checks the captured C-family identifier tokens before
accepting a rename proposal and refuses an existing target spelling with
`c.renameTargetIdentifierPresent`. `CTokenizer` excludes comments and
string/character literals; the guard only refuses and never creates edits. It
is intentionally conservative: same-spelled identifiers in a safe scope or a
separate C namespace may also be refused. Scope-aware collision/shadowing/
linkage analysis remains open.

The one-scenario/four-step Story passes through library, daemon and MCP. All
surfaces return the exact refusal code, preserve every project byte and create
no transaction. The original exact-header-preview/name-only-refusal outline
was separately rerun against the updated feature hash and both examples/eight
steps still pass. The current feature hash is
`4b566eb0ee8954d63f6b4d29fe6fc1f906ba6cdac439d76d7ac7bd739a83599e`.
Receipts, bounded pre/post inventories, source-view manifests, Cucumber JSON,
JUnit XML and execution summaries are in
`.refactorkit/runs/c-semantic-rename-collision-r001/` and
`.refactorkit/runs/c-semantic-rename-exact-selection-r002/`. Both analyzer
inventories use one shared runner, four exact post-edit step matches, zero
ambiguities and zero duplicate expressions. CLI Story checks pass with Java 21,
Gradle offline and the pinned private Clang/clangd/clang-format 22.1.8 toolchain.
This adds one bounded C06 refusal cell; complete C06 and C01-C17 release
acceptance remain open.


### C06 C tag-namespace-only rename target — 2026-10-22

The previous token guard refused a valid C rename when the requested target
spelling appeared only as `struct`, `union` or `enum` tag. The new
`CTargetNameCollisionPolicy` treats those direct tag-name tokens as separate
from ordinary function/variable names only in captured `c` sources and outside
preprocessor directives. Ordinary identifiers, C++ type names, macro/directive
occurrences, labels, members and uncertain contexts still refuse with
`c.renameTargetIdentifierPresent`; this does not claim scope/linkage or full
binding analysis.

The behavioral RED reproduced the prior false refusal. The source-built GREEN
Story proves exact header-prototype/definition/call edits through library,
daemon and MCP while preserving `struct compute`, every project byte and the
absence of a transaction. Five approved Kotest specifications pass for
tag-only, comment/literal, ordinary collision, directive and C++ boundaries.
The earlier exact-selection outline (2 examples/8 steps) and same-TU ordinary
collision Story (1/4) were each rerun separately against feature hash
`a54a4ec7cc785f8bb1c9a74303a45ad17860d3c3925a25713216263c041c06fc`; all
three runs are green. Fresh pre/post inventories report one shared runner, no
ambiguity/duplicates, and four exact post-edit step reuses. The nine-file
implementation/docs slice uses the user's approved numeric 10-file BDD override.
Receipts, analyzer inventories, source manifests and test reports are in
`.refactorkit/runs/c-semantic-rename-tag-namespace-r001/`,
`.refactorkit/runs/c-semantic-rename-collision-r002/`, and
`.refactorkit/runs/c-semantic-rename-exact-selection-r003/`. Broader C06 family,
scope, linkage, macro, consumer-closure, managed-Apply, C16 and C17 acceptance
remain open.

### IDE Java changeVisibility — 2026-10-24

Added a bounded `changeVisibility` operation that promotes one exact JDT-bound
concrete method to `public`. The selected declaration must belong directly to a
top-level ordinary class without a declared superclass, interfaces or type
parameters. Annotated/generated declarations refuse; instance methods must be
final (or belong to a final class), while static methods are admitted directly.
The planner stages the modifier edit, proves that the same JDT method binding is
public, checks diagnostic deltas, and refuses non-final instance methods with
unproven override paths. The HIGH-risk preview warns that public and reflective
API visibility expands. Daemon and MCP expose preview, managed Apply, and exact
rollback; this does not qualify standard LSP, dirty buffers, or packaged/native
IDE consumers.

`REQ-JAVA-CHANGE-VISIBILITY-001#promote-a-final-private-method-to-public-and-rollback`
passes capability discovery, typed refusal, diagnostics, daemon/MCP Apply, and
rollback. Its §18.6 receipt and inventories are under
`.refactorkit/runs/ide-change-visibility-r001/`.

### Native IDE query-session report reconciled with existing acceptance — 2026-10-26

The IDE consumer reports that, after successful two-module Maven `project.context`
v2 acquisition with matching attestations, the read-only `definition` query ended
the session without a JSON-RPC response. Native GUI run artifacts `run-5FGgBw`
and `run-zQSY8e` identify the installed daemon (v0.7.0, SHA-256
`21dcd303596607fd8287d30f0a674a925917856215f10dbb32c8e6e725262219`) and capture
RPC id 6 for the unanswered definition query. The two runs report session errors
after 282 ms and 356 ms, and their lifecycle traces show overlapping Monaco
requests and cancellation. The IDE withdrew context and declined the next
`implementations` request pending resynchronization. This is attached to the
existing `REQ-JAVA-QUERY-CONTEXT-001` scenario, with no duplicate feature or
requirement.

The existing scenario was strengthened to assert that both query responses
preserve the SDK platform identities from `project.context`, alongside context
hash/schema. The selected source-built daemon scenario passes (1 passed, 2 sibling
scenarios skipped); the packaged daemon scenario also passes (1 passed, 2 skipped).
Both reports have zero failures/errors and preserve source/POM bytes. This verifies
the RefactorKit source and packaged NDJSON paths but does not reproduce the native
IDE report. Separate diagnostic probes against the exact installed daemon SHA
cancelled definition queries at 40 ms and 100 ms; the daemon returned JSON-RPC
`-32800` by 43 ms and 136 ms, and a following `server.version` succeeded in each
same process. A heavier synthetic reactor with 502 Java sources had a 3,255 ms
uncancelled definition query; cancellation at 100 ms returned `-32800` by 103 ms
and `server.version` still succeeded. This does not exercise the native GUI bridge.
Inspection of the IDE Rust transport shows cancellation sends
`intelligence.cancel` and retires the daemon if no reply arrives within 250 ms.
In `run-zQSY8e`, the pre-query context verification took 784 ms, the query failed
at 356 ms, and the provider returned 276 ms after the recorded cancellation.
The later instrumented native diagnostic `run-0gOKAI` records the cancel write at
125 ms after query dispatch and `cancellation_deadline` at 375 ms total, with the
daemon process still running and no JSON-RPC response for the query. Its GUI
scenario then failed at a 20-second wait for the `Helper.java` tab; it is
diagnostic evidence, not passing native acceptance. This confirms deadline-driven
retirement of a live child but leaves open whether the daemon received the cancel
and why its query response was late.

The final IDE no-bundle build uses a one-second cancellation-acknowledgement
bound. Its native run `run-CvDadq` passed the full scenario (1 scenario, 29/29
steps): both in-flight cancellations received daemon error `-32800`; after each,
the IDE reacquired context and a following definition query returned `ready` on
the same session. The app closed cleanly without forced termination. The IDE
agent also reports five focused Rust tests, `rustfmt`, `git diff --check`, and
`pnpm validate:docs` passing. A follow-up IDE report says the rebuilt
`javaCursorNavigationNativeGui` profile also passes (1 scenario, 29 steps) with
the installed RefactorKit 0.7.0 daemon, JDT LS 1.62 and GraalVM JDK 25: all 15
queries received responses, including one acknowledged cancellation (`-32800`),
and later definition/implementations queries succeeded on the still-usable
session. This corroborates the current IDE transport behavior for that Linux
profile; it does not establish a provider defect in RefactorKit. The run artifacts
remain in the IDE consumer workspace. This verifies the reported session-loss
path on one Linux x86-64 release-no-bundle build; it does not measure exact provider
cancellation latency or qualify packaged/other-platform delivery. Keep broader
integration qualification `actionable`. Current RefactorKit source reacquires
effective context for each `javaContext` query, including fresh workspace scans
without a cancellation token; this remains a latency hypothesis, not proven
against the installed v0.7.0 daemon's source revision. Failure, comparison and

### Daemon query worker isolation after a non-fatal handler error — 2026-10-26

The existing single-worker daemon scheduler converted `Exception` failures to
JSON-RPC `INTERNAL_ERROR`, but an `AssertionError` or other non-fatal `Error`
escaped the worker loop. That could leave the session open with queued requests
never answered. The scheduler now returns an internal error for non-fatal
`Throwable` failures and continues processing; `VirtualMachineError` and
`ThreadDeath` remain fatal and are rethrown.

The new one-scenario Story first produced a genuine tests-only RED: the injected
query-handler `AssertionError` killed the worker before a response, and the
follow-up was stranded. It now passes one scenario and four steps, and the full
daemon module suite passes 64 tests with zero failures, errors or skips,
including both existing scheduler tests. Pre/post analyzer inventories show
four exact step reuses, no new post-edit glue, no ambiguity and no effective
duplicate expressions. The focused manifest validator passes on an isolated
source view.
The full repository validator still reports existing duplicate identities in
archived run copies and acceptance fixtures; the new requirement ID is unique
under `features/`, and the finding does not match this Story.

This closes only that scheduler isolation path; it does not reproduce the
reported native IDE query loss, prove the installed artifact is current, or
qualify JVM-fatal recovery. The native consumer report remains actionable, and
the source-built and packaged session-continuity scenarios remain separate
positive evidence. Evidence is under
`.refactorkit/runs/daemon-request-error-isolation-r001/`.

After this scheduler change, the existing packaged daemon query-continuity
scenario also passed again: one selected scenario, two sibling scenarios
skipped. It rebuilt the self-contained `refactorkitRuntimeDist` from the current
tree and served `definition` plus `implementations` in the same daemon session.
The local daemon JAR still carries version `0.7.0`; this is current-tree package
plumbing evidence, not a released or versioned 0.8.0 candidate and not a native
IDE run. The packaged test does not inject the non-fatal handler error.

### C include-graph defensive value boundary — 2026-10-26

A retained SpotBugs report in `.refactorkit/runs/v080-regression-current-r001/`
flags `CIncludeDirective.diagnostics` and `CIncludeGraph` collections as potentially
externally mutable. Both models now copy caller collections into unmodifiable
snapshots while preserving constructor names, value equality, `copy` and
destructuring behavior. The new pure-domain Kotest Spec verifies constructor and
`copy` input isolation plus rejected mutation through getters; it passes 2/2 cases.
The existing include-graph builder regression passes 6/6 cases. Hash-bound XML and
source evidence are under
`.refactorkit/runs/c-include-graph-immutability-r001/`. This corrects the bounded
model boundary only. SpotBugs was not rerun: required artifacts are not in the
offline cache and online resolution remains unapproved. Other findings, full C02-C17
qualification and 0.8.0 release acceptance remain open.

### C include-graph duplicate-edge and root-path guards — 2026-10-26

The existing C SpotBugs log also identifies a possible null dereference when
ownership classification receives a filesystem root with no filename. The classifier
is now a pure function that maps that path to `UNKNOWN` without throwing. The graph
constructor's former edge check compared an expression with itself and could never
reject duplicates; it now rejects duplicate edge identities while retaining repeated
includes on distinct source lines. The same Kotest Spec covers both boundaries. Its
four cases and the six existing builder regressions pass; hash-bound reports and
sources are under `.refactorkit/runs/c-include-graph-immutability-r002/`. This is
bounded C model evidence only; a fresh SpotBugs run, remaining findings, C02-C17
qualification and 0.8.0 release acceptance remain open.

The retained C static-analysis report flags `CCompilationUnit.arguments`,

unmodifiable snapshots; `CCompilationDatabase.units` receives the same
protection because it is the enclosing captured command set. Both public value
models retain their constructor names, equality, hash code, `copy`, destructuring
and string representation. The pure-domain Kotest Spec passes 2/2 cases, the
compilation database parser regressions pass 10/10, and provider regressions pass
5/5. Hash-bound reports and sources are under

### C Clang resource snapshot constructor boundary — 2026-10-26

The retained SpotBugs report flags `ClangResourceDirectorySnapshot.files` and
`directories` because the public data-class constructor and `copy()` can retain
mutable caller lists. The model now snapshots both collections itself, so
immutability does not depend on construction through `ClangResourceDirectoryFacts`.
Its pure-domain Spec now covers direct construction, `copy`, immutable getters,
destructuring and prior value semantics; all 8 cases pass. This hardens only the
captured resource value boundary. Hash-bound full-suite evidence is recorded in
the next module-run entry; remaining static-analysis findings and release gates
remain open.

for named or dynamic queries, explicit entity mappings, XML/YAML/properties,
migrations, generated metamodels, reflection, or external consumers. Therefore
the IDE's disabled Apply is the intended safe outcome for this shape, not a
package-release qualification. Keep the field-rename requirement `@partial`;
expand provider coverage and its completeness tests under that same
requirement before considering managed Apply. Native IDE acceptance and overall
0.8.0 qualification remain open.
